# TASKS — Kokonada

The single running list of everything still open in this repo. **If it is not written here, it does not exist.**
A task that lives only in a conversation is already lost — that is what this file exists to stop.

**Last updated:** 2026-10-01
**Next free IDs:** `BE-084` · `MB-084` · `UI-064` · `OPS-094` · `ARCH-013` · `DAN-048`

---

## How this file is maintained — read this, every session

**Read `TASKS.md` at session start, right after `CLAUDE.md` and the `docs/ORCHESTRATOR_FABLE.md` session-start protocol. Recommend the next job from it.**

**1 · Capture immediately, not later.**
The moment Daniel says something should happen afterwards — "we'll do that next", "remind me", "not now", "אחר כך" — write the row *before* continuing the current work. Not at the end of the session. Not "I'll remember".

**2 · When the section is unclear, use `## Inbox`.**
Quote his own words verbatim and move on. Never drop a task because it did not fit a heading. Sort the inbox at the start of the next session.

**3 · Close only on evidence.**
A row moves to the archive when the work is done **and proven** — a passing test, a real on-device run, a commit hash. Never on intent, never on "should work", never on a green mock. If it is half done, leave it open and add what is left.

**4 · Never silently delete a row.**
If a task becomes irrelevant, move it to the archive marked `dropped` with one line of why. The archive is how we avoid re-litigating the same question in three weeks.

**5 · Every seeded row is a claim, not truth.**
Rows carrying `#verify` were written from outside the code and have not been proven against the tree. Before acting on one: challenge the assumption, prove each claim against the actual files, fix what is wrong, add what is missing. **Nothing gets deleted or changed on an unverified assumption.** If a claim cannot be proven either way, mark it `#inconclusive`, leave the code alone, and say so. Drop the `#verify` tag only once you have checked it yourself and cite the `file:line` you checked.

**6 · Update this file in the same commit as the work.**
That is what keeps it from drifting. Commit message stays short and single-line, no body, no trailers, no attribution of any kind (`CLAUDE.md` → Attribution policy).

**7 · IDs are never reused.**
Take the next free ID from the header and bump it there in the same edit.

**8 · Tasks go here, not into a new `.md` file.**
Daniel does not want the repo flooded with markdown. A task gets a row here; only a genuinely large piece of work earns its own doc under `docs/plans/`, and then this file carries the row that links to it.

### Row format

```
- [ ] `BE-004` `P2` One sentence — what is wrong or what must exist.
  - where: `path/to/file.js:41` · done when: the observable condition · src: where this came from · added: YYYY-MM-DD
```

**Priorities:** `P0` blocking or a live security/data risk · `P1` next up · `P2` should happen · `P3` nice to have.
**Tags:** `#verify` unproven claim · `#inconclusive` checked, could not be settled · `#blocked` waiting on something named in the row.

## ⚠ AT THE TOP, BECAUSE IT IS ALREADY IN FORCE

- [ ] `DAN-010` `P2` **WITH COUNSEL — not blocking. LEGAL: is Kokonada a high-risk emotion recognition system under the EU AI Act?** Daniel's and counsel's; not a build-side item and not closeable by a design change. `#counsel` `#not-blocking`
  - **Re-tiered P0 → P2 on 2026-09-01, and the reason is a calendar correction, not a downgrade of seriousness.** The paragraph below said the Annex III high-risk obligations "applied from 2 August 2026 — in force, not upcoming." **That is no longer true.** Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (the Digital Omnibus on AI, OJ 24 July 2026, in force 27 July 2026) **deferred Annex III to 2 December 2027** — roughly fifteen months out — while leaving the **Art. 50 transparency duty at 2 August 2026**. Art. 50 is the one with a live date, and it is already tracked separately as `UI-005`.
  - The dossier for counsel is prepared: `docs/plans/LEGAL_AI_ACT_EMOTION_RECOGNITION.md`, written to be read cold, with the two-calendar table at the top. Nothing further is owed by this repo until counsel rules.
  - **The obligations applied from 2 August 2026. This is in force, not upcoming.** Art. 3(39) covers inferring emotions from biometric data; Art. 3(34) defines biometric data as processing of "physical, physiological or behavioural characteristics" and **omits GDPR Art. 4(14)'s unique-identification limb**, so HR/HRV appear to qualify where they would not under GDPR. Annex III(1)(c) makes emotion recognition high-risk; Art. 6(2) applies it; Art. 113(c) defers **only Art. 6(1)**, not 6(2). Recital 18 excludes "physical states, such as pain or fatigue" — but our outputs are valence-axis affective states ("Resting but content", `tense-but-positive`) and the token is literally `emotionAccent`.
  - **The full write-up is `docs/plans/LEGAL_AI_ACT_EMOTION_RECOGNITION.md`** — articles quoted, the reasoning chain, the counter-argument stated at its strongest, what could not be verified, and the Chapter III obligations that would attach. Written to be read cold by a lawyer.
  - **Unverified and decisive:** whether HR/HRV is Art. 3(34) biometric data is genuinely contested, and the Commission's **May 2026 Annex III guidance could not be retrieved**. That document is the first thing counsel should obtain; everything else follows from it.
  - **Already failing regardless of the high-risk question:** Art. 50(3)/(5) require informing people that an emotion recognition system is operating, "in a clear and distinguishable manner at the latest at the time of the first interaction". Our only such line sits inside collapsed §3 of the consent notice and does not say the system infers emotion.
  - **Do not design around it.** Removing the word "emotion" from the UI while the system still infers affective states from physiological data changes nothing legally and costs the product its honesty.
  - **A `regulatory-researcher` agent (`OPS-020`) has been created and its first assignment is this row.** It retrieves primary sources, maps which obligations attach under each classification, and keeps unverified items marked unverified. **Its output is preparation for a lawyer, not a substitute for one, and must never be read as closing this question.** It is explicitly forbidden from returning a verdict on a question of law. Its dossier attaches to `docs/plans/LEGAL_AI_ACT_EMOTION_RECOGNITION.md`; this row stays open until counsel answers.
  - **Blocked on a human, not on work.** Nothing the build team does closes this.
  - src: `compliance-auditor` re-audit of the corrected canvas, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
  - **update 2026-09-24:** counsel engagement now bundles this question with the Track H question lists → `DAN-014` (this question is `brief_regulatory` Q6–Q7 and `r_regulatory` Q8).
  - **update 2026-09-26:** `regulatory-researcher` (H1 review) retrieved Art. 50 Guidelines C(2026) 5054 para 102: "all emotion recognition systems are also classified as high-risk" (Annex III 1(c)). If the affect engine is emotion recognition (`backend/app/agents/runtime/knowledge/stateTaxonomy.js`, whose domains include `emotional` and `stress`), the high-risk duties attach from the Annex III date (reported as 2 December 2027; the Official Journal text was not retrieved). Nothing changes here: it is counsel's question, already in `DAN-014`.

## Resume — read this first (written 2026-08-28)

> **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-23.** Everything parked below has landed: CI is back, `remove/web-frontend` merged as PR #187, and `BE-001`/`BE-003` closed with it. Kept for history only.

**Everything is blocked on one external fact: the GitHub Actions budget is $0 with stop-usage at 100%, so NO PR gets CI until the quota resets on 1 SEPTEMBER 2026.** Runs are refused at dispatch — six jobs die in 3–4 seconds with `steps=0`, no failed step, because no step ever ran. A red like that says nothing about the code. `OPS-015` stops the burn recurring next cycle; it cannot restore this cycle.

**What is PARKED, deliberately, not stalled:**
- **Branch `remove/web-frontend`** — rebased onto `06af5d6`, intact, **do not land it**. It carries 157 `frontend/` deletions + the two dead pairing routes + the two browser-only YouTube endpoints. Nothing is committed; the work sits in the worktree index.
- **The open A-versus-B call on that branch** (see `ARCH-002`): whether to fold `BE-001` + `BE-003` + the `cors()` removal into the same commit, or defer them. **Do not decide this before CI is available.** The only reason it was hard is that merging a 157-file deletion *plus* a security-boundary redesign unverified is a bad trade; with CI that trade disappears.

**What is DONE and RUNNING in production:** `BE-008` (cross-site WebSocket hijacking — closed) and `BE-009` (cookie auth plane retired). Confirmed live by behavioural probe, not by a deployment id — see `BE-012` for the copy-paste `curl` and the reason the id is not the sha.

**First job when CI returns, in order:**
1. Re-run CI on `main` (`06af5d6`) and confirm the `OPS-015` gate behaves — `mobile-ios-build` should report **`skipped`**, not `success`, on a non-iOS change. That is the first genuine verification of that commit.
2. Then make the A/B call on branch B with real CI in hand.
3. `BE-001` + `BE-003` must land **before** any Vercel teardown — see `DAN-002`.

**Standing traps to carry, all learned the hard way today:** a green check here has meant nothing in four distinct ways (`OPS-010` filtered job, `OPS-011` stacked PR, `OPS-013` real app never assembled, `OPS-014` assertions loose enough to pass on the bug). A Railway deployment id is not a commit sha. "Nothing broke" and "the new build is running" are different questions needing different tests.

## Backend

- [x] `BE-012` `P0` **CLOSED 2026-08-28 — the fix is deployed and RUNNING, confirmed by probing the live service.** Daniel toggled "Wait for CI" off, deployed, and turned it back on. `BE-008`/`BE-009` are live: the cross-site WebSocket hole is closed in production.
  - **How it was verified, because the obvious method did not work.** The identifiers Railway showed (`6497751a`, then `cb702653`) are **deployment IDs, not git commit shas** — `git cat-file -e` resolves neither as an object in this repo, and Railway's UI truncates them into something that looks exactly like a sha. Comparing them against `main` is meaningless, and doing so would have produced a confident wrong answer in either direction. **Never verify a Railway deploy by the id on the deployment card; read the source commit, or use `OPS-018` once it lands.**
  - ### ⚠️ STANDING INSTRUMENT — how to identify a deployed build, copy-paste
    **A Railway deployment ID looks exactly like a git commit sha and resolves as neither.** `git cat-file -e cb702653^{commit}` and `6497751a` both fail: no such object. Comparing a deployment ID against `main` is not a weak check, it is a meaningless one, and it returns a confident answer either way. **Never verify a deploy from the id on the deployment card.** Read the *source commit* on the deployment, or use the probe below.
    ```bash
    API=https://kokonada-backend-production.up.railway.app
    curl -s -H "Cookie: kokonada_token=garbage.not.a.jwt" "$API/api/auth/me"
    ```
    | response | meaning |
    |---|---|
    | `{"error":"Authentication required"}` | **post-`BE-009`** — cookies are ignored entirely. The cookie plane is retired and the CSWSH fix is live. |
    | `{"error":"Invalid or expired token"}` | **pre-`BE-009`** — the build still READS the cookie and fails verifying it. The hole is open. |
    Needs no credential, no dashboard and no Railway access. It works because the two builds take different branches in `middleware/auth.js` (`:52` vs `:79`) for the same input. **`OPS-018` retires this whole question** — once `/health` carries a build identifier, "what is running" is one GET and no behavioural inference is required.
  - ### Two different questions, two different tests — do not let one stand in for the other
    **"Nothing broke"** and **"the new build is running"** are separate, and only the second was ever in doubt here. The five-step phone test answers the first and **cannot** answer the second: sign-in, live HR and playlist generation all work on *either* build, because the cookie plane worked too. A green phone test on the old build looks identical to a green phone test on the new one. Behaviour that is unchanged by design cannot identify which design is deployed — that needs an input where the two builds provably differ, which is what the probe above supplies.
  - ~~`main` AND THE CODE ACTUALLY SERVING REQUESTS ARE DIFFERENT CODE TODAY.~~ The CSWSH fix is merged but NOT RUNNING — the any-origin authenticated socket is still open in production.** Railway's active deployment is `6497751a` (2026-08-26 20:08 GMT+3, *"Wave4 W4-007+W4-008 … (#180)"*). `main` is `e22de37` (`BE-008`/`BE-009`). Nothing that was merged yesterday evening or later is live.
  - **Why it stuck:** the backend deploys through Railway's **native GitHub integration** with **"auto-deploy on push" + "wait for CI" enabled** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:241-247`). The GitHub **Actions budget is $0 with stop-usage at 100%**, so every run is refused at dispatch with `steps=0` — CI can never go green, so the deploy gate never opens. **The CI consumption fix (`OPS-015`) does NOT unstick this**: the budget is already exhausted for the cycle, so runs are refused regardless of workflow content. Nothing deploys before the **September 1** reset without intervention.
  - **⚠️ "Redeploy" in Railway does NOT fix this — it re-runs THAT deployment's commit.** Clicking Redeploy on `6497751a` redeploys `6497751a`, i.e. the same pre-`BE-009` build with the hole still open, while looking like it worked.
  - **Recommended fix (Daniel only, portal action):** Railway → backend service → **Settings → Source / Deploy triggers** → turn **OFF "Wait for CI"** → let it deploy the current `main` head → **confirm the new deployment's source commit reads `e22de37`, not `6497751a`** → turn **"Wait for CI" back ON**. Cost: while it is off, any push to `main` deploys unverified — so do it in one sitting and re-enable immediately.
  - **Do NOT use `railway up`.** Already learned and recorded at `ci.yml:244-247`: it fights the service's Root Directory (`/backend`), fails the build in ~2s, and that failed check then blocks the native deploy.
  - **`/health` cannot answer "what is running"** — it returns `{"status":"ok","ts":…}` with no version or commit. That is `OPS-018`. Railway's deployment page is the only authority today.
  - **The phone test in `BE-009` must NOT be run until the running sha is `e22de37`** — testing the old build proves nothing.
  - done when: Railway's active deployment shows `e22de37` or later, and "Wait for CI" is back on · src: deployment state confirmed by Daniel, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
- [x] `BE-008` `P0` ~~CROSS-SITE WEBSOCKET HIJACKING — live in production right now. Any website on the internet can open a fully authenticated duplex socket for any logged-in user and read back Art.9 biometric data.~~ **CLOSED ON EVIDENCE 2026-09-02 — the hole is shut on `main` (PR #184) and verified in this merge.** `backend/app/sockets/index.js` now takes `handshake.auth.token` ONLY — the raw `Cookie:` fallback is deleted and every remaining mention in that file is explanatory comment. The `done when` condition is met: `backend/tests/socket.crossSite.test.js` exists and the cross-site handshake is REJECTED, and the `socket.auth.test.js` case that used to prove the hole is INVERTED rather than deleted, so the old behaviour cannot return unnoticed. This is the most serious defect surfaced in this effort, and it lands directly on the `<locked_decisions>` zero-knowledge-biometrics guarantee.
  - **The mechanism, four facts that only compose into a hole together:** (1) `backend/app/sockets/index.js:27-33` falls back to parsing the raw `Cookie:` header when `handshake.auth.token` is absent. (2) `backend/app/utils/jwt.js:17` sets the session cookie `sameSite:'none', secure:true` in production, so the browser attaches it to cross-site requests. (3) WebSocket upgrades are **exempt from the same-origin policy** — there is no preflight and no ACAO check to fail. (4) `/socket.io/` never reaches Express middleware at all: Engine.IO intercepts the `request`/`upgrade` listeners ahead of Express, so `csrfOriginGuard` (mounted at `/api/`, `app/index.js:95`) can never see the handshake. `engine.io/build/server.js:61-63` wires `opts.cors` as the same `cors` npm middleware, which sets headers and calls `next()` — it does **not** reject the upgrade.
  - **Impact:** `io('https://…railway.app', {transports:['websocket'], withCredentials:true})` from `evil.example` yields an authenticated socket able to emit `biometric_push`, `emotion_update`, `request_playlist`, `playback_event`, `track_skipped` (`sockets/biometricHandler.js:1772-1876`) and read every payload emitted back.
  - **A PASSING TEST SITS ON TOP OF THE MECHANISM.** `backend/tests/socket.auth.test.js:193-197` — *"accepts a valid session cookie when no handshake.auth.token is present"* — proves it over a real WebSocket (`transports:['websocket']` :60, `extraHeaders:{Cookie:…}` :63). The only thing it omits is an `Origin` header, and no code path reads one. The suite is not blind here; it is *documenting* the hole as intended behaviour.
  - fix: delete the cookie fallback — `handshake.auth.token` only. Both real clients already use it (`mobile/.../net/socketFactory.ts:17`); the web client that needed `withCredentials` is being deleted. Lands in `BE-009`.
  - done when: `backend/tests/socket.crossSite.test.js` exists (clone `socket.auth.test.js:58-67`, add `Origin: 'https://evil.example'`) and the handshake is REJECTED. It currently connects — the test is RED today and should be. · src: `resilience-auditor`, 2026-08-26 · added: 2026-08-26

Cookie-borne auth reaches every state-changing route: `middleware/auth.js:36` reads `req.cookies[COOKIE_NAME]` **before** the Bearer header, and `auth.middleware.test.js:138-155` pins that the cookie WINS when both are present. Deleting `frontend/` does not retire it — `authController.js:178` sets the cookie on **every refresh with no client check**, and the mobile app's own session refresh calls that endpoint (`mobile/.../auth/session.ts:25-28`).

- [ ] `BE-014` `P2` **`/auth/refresh` shares the anti-credential-stuffing budget with sign-in, so a broken session can lock a user out of the repair path.** `authLimiter` is `max: 10` per 15 min, **IP-keyed** (no `keyGenerator`), and covers `/google`, `/apple`, `/signup`, `/login` **and `/refresh`** (`routes/auth.js:11-17`). `apiClient.ts:47-48` fires a refresh on *every* 401 and does not retry on failure, so a client whose refresh token is dead burns all ten slots in seconds — and then cannot sign in for 15 minutes to fix it. `#inconclusive`
  - **NOT the observed cause of the 2026-08-27 429, and severity is rated on that.** Access TTL is `AUTH_ACCESS_TTL || '15m'` (`tokenService.js:17`) against a 15-minute limiter window, so a *healthy* client refreshes once per window and consumes **1 of 10**. My earlier "normal app usage consumes the sign-in allowance" was wrong and is corrected here. The 429 was most likely repeated sign-in taps during testing. Consistent with the app working normally now: a valid access token means no 401s, no refreshes, zero limiter consumption.
  - **UNRECOVERABLE FROM LOGS — this is not waiting on Daniel.** There is **no HTTP access logger anywhere in the backend**: no `morgan`/`pino`/`winston` in `package.json`, no logging middleware in the `app.use` chain (`index.js:40-103`), and **nothing in `app/middleware/` logs at all**. `apiLimiter` sits at `index.js:94`, *ahead of every router*, and `express-rate-limit` answers 429 from inside it without calling `next()` — so a rate-limited request never reaches a controller, and controllers are the only things that log. **A 429'd request produces zero log output by construction.** No amount of Railway access would answer which endpoints consumed those ten requests. Reopen only if `OPS-019` lands and the pattern recurs.
  - **The obvious fix has a gap, checked before proposing it.** Per-user keying does *not* work: refresh tokens are opaque `krt_` random bytes hashed at rest (`tokenService.js:13,37`), so the user is only knowable after a DB lookup *inside* the handler, while a `keyGenerator` runs before that. A missing/expired/forged token yields no derivable user at all — every such request would collide on one null key, which is worse than IP keying.
  - **Corrected design:** give `/refresh` its **own IP-keyed limiter with a higher ceiling**, separate from the four credential endpoints. No user key, no gap, and a refresh storm can no longer consume the sign-in budget. Per-user accounting, if ever wanted, belongs inside the handler after validation where the user is known.
  - done when: `/refresh` no longer shares a limiter with `/login`/`/signup`/`/google`/`/apple`, pinned by a test · src: 429 investigation, 2026-08-27; corrected against code 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
  - **update 2026-09-24:** the client turns a 429 on `/refresh` into a lost session — any non-ok refresh deletes the stored tokens (`MB-019`).
- [ ] `BE-013` `P1` **`dailyReadiness` is a consumer with no producer — give it a real source.** It is read at `medicalProfileService.js:89` (`lowReadinessLowSteps`) and listed in `PROFILE_SCALAR_METRICS` at `:246`, but **nothing anywhere writes it**: `normalizeGarminSummaries` never emits it and a grep for `readiness` under `services/wearable/` returns zero. Decided 2026-08-26: it is not deleted and it does not stay sourceless.
  - **It cannot be Garmin.** Garmin's Health API supplies no readiness metric of any kind (verified against their live product page), and the Garmin lane is blocked upstream anyway → `DAN-009`. The comments calling it "Garmin Training Readiness" (`medicalProfileService.js:246`) and "Garmin/Whoop readiness score" (`models/MedicalProfile.js:65`) are both wrong and should go with the fix.
  - **The available source is our own Health Connect lane.** HRV, resting heart rate and sleep stages are exactly the conventional readiness inputs, we already read all three at consent v1, and `medicalProfileService` already computes the per-user medians a readiness score needs as its baseline. That makes it **Kokonada's derived metric with a stated method**, not a vendor's — which also answers Apple 1.4.1 ("clearly disclose data and methodology to support accuracy claims relating to health measurements").
  - **Route to `architect` first** — the score's definition is a modelling decision, not an implementation detail, and it must not reuse Garmin's feature name. Also reconcile the other session's in-flight `b0c7a82`/`72a5079`, which disclosed and gated "Garmin Training Readiness" without ever wiring an ingest path.

## Mobile

- [ ] `MB-008` `P1` **YouTube connect has no mobile caller — after `frontend/` is deleted it becomes an unbuilt entry point, exactly like Garmin.** Nothing in `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src` calls `GET /api/integrations/youtube/connect`. Mobile can only *disconnect* (`profileController.ts:73` → `apiDelete('/api/integrations/youtube/disconnect')`) and *display status* (`ProfileScreen.tsx:209,290-293`). The web `IntegrationsPage` was the only initiator and it dies with `ARCH-002`. `#verify`
  - **The UI is already honest about it** — `ProfileScreen.tsx:292` renders `statusWord={youtubeConnected ? 'Connected' : 'Not yet available'}`. So this is a documented gap, not a lie to the user, and no screen starts claiming something false when the web goes.
  - **RESERVED, not DEAD** — a shipped surface still promises the flow, and the backend half is complete: `youtubeConnect` (`integrationsController.js:315`) and `youtubeCallback` (`:333`) survive `ARCH-002` and already carry the `returnTo=app` deep-link branch (`:319, :327, :337, :363`). **This is strictly less work than Garmin (`BE-003`), which needs the app branch built as well — YouTube needs only the mobile entry point.**
  - **Consequence for the teardown, and it is why this row exists now:** step 3 of `DAN-002`'s redirect repoint says "verify a real YouTube connect completes". Until this lands there is **no client able to start one**, so that verification cannot be performed from the app. Either accept the repoint unverified (low risk — the value is byte-identical in shape to Spotify's working one), or verify by hitting `GET /api/integrations/youtube/connect` directly with a valid session and following the redirect in a browser.
  - where: mobile has no caller; the pattern to copy is Spotify's at `ProfileScreen.tsx:113` (`…/spotify/connect?ct=…&returnTo=app`) · done when: a mobile entry point starts the flow and the callback deep-links back into the app · src: web-removal analysis, 2026-08-27 · added: 2026-08-27

Either rebuild the profile from YouTube after a Spotify disconnect, or say so in the copy.
  - **update 2026-10-01 — do not build it while `DEC-122` stands:** the owner ruled on 2026-10-01 that connecting YouTube is sealed in code on the server (`DEC-122`), so no mobile entry point is to be built; on `fix/hx3-youtube` connect and callback answer `youtube_unavailable` whatever the app sends (`backend/app/controllers/integrationsController.js:347-352,377-380`). This row waits on `ARCH-012`: D deletes the YouTube code at P3, and lifting the seal first needs its prerequisites. The lines re-checked by the scribe on `bbae791`.

- [ ] `MB-048` `P2` **The phone never learns that the server refused or replaced its live heart-rate credential: `pushLiveHr` returns the status, and neither tier acts on a 401, a 403 `consent_required`/`consent_stale` or a 409 `{live:false}`.** The server keeps one `whr_` per user and every mint overwrites it, so a sign-in on a second phone — every sign-in mints on PR #193 — voids the first phone's credential, which then pushes into 401 until its own next sign-in. After a withdrawal on another phone, a lost withdraw response or the P5 version bump, the feed keeps reading and sending refused readings until the next foreground or boot.

- [ ] `MB-049` `P2` **Withdrawing health consent takes about 26 s, and the phone keeps collecting until the server answers: the erasure runs inside the request, and the device stops the feed only after a successful response.** The H2 gate measured `POST /api/consent/withdraw` at 26 042 ms against 475 ms for the grant, with the Bluetooth scan still running about 30 s after the user confirmed. On a dropped connection the user sees "Could not withdraw consent" although the server has committed it; a 5xx leaves the feed running with no error on the card. Art. 7(3): withdrawing must be as easy as giving consent.

## Design & UI

- [ ] `UI-001` `P1` "Delete my account" and "Withdraw health consent" must be **real, reachable actions** on the You screen — a standing requirement across every canvas stage, explicitly not to be satisfied by trimming copy. `#verify`
  - where: You / Profile screen, `docs/SCREENS.md` · done when: both actions exist in the shipped app and do what they say, verified on device · src: `docs/STAGE_2_6_PROMPT.md`, `docs/STAGE_2_END_PROMPT.md` · added: 2026-08-26
- [ ] `UI-002` `P2` Confirm every reviewed screen carries an explicit `designer` **SHIP** verdict, or record why it does not — `CLAUDE.md` forbids merging a screen without one. `#verify`
  - where: the twelve reviews in `docs/review/01-splash.html` … `12-profile.html` · done when: one line per screen, SHIP or a named reason · src: `docs/STAGE_2_END_PROMPT.md` handover requirement · added: 2026-08-26

- [ ] `UI-003` `P1` **Open canvas items from the six-audit compliance pass (2026-08-26/27).** Most of that pass is closed on the boards; these are the ones still outstanding, each already written up in full. `#blocked` on nothing — they are ordinary design work.
  - **Cover art is required, and the boards paint a gradient.** Spotify Developer Policy II.5: no playback of Spotify Content without showing relevant cover art. Every mini-player artwork tile is a mood gradient. Convert to labelled cover-art slots, the same way the marks were, and pin "the gradient placeholder may never ship" into the per-screen DoD. Artwork radii 20px/10px must become 4px/8px. → `docs/plans/DEV_SPOTIFY_CANVAS_ASSETS.md`
  - **The YouTube icon slot needs the shipped half.** The Components board now states the rule and carries both slots; the app still has no provider discriminator on the attribution slot. → `docs/plans/HALT_YOUTUBE_ATTRIBUTION_AND_REVOCATION.md` YT-2
  - **Consent first layer.** Purpose and controller identity still sit inside collapsed sections, and the accordion closes one to open another while claiming "this is the whole notice". EDPB 05/2020 para 64 wants both visible before the consent action. Promote them; allow multiple sections open.
  - **The notice is Android-only.** `BiometricLog.source` includes `apple_health`; on iOS the copy misdescribes both the source and the flow. Needs platform-conditional copy. (The Google-brand naming half is done.)
  - **`You.dc.html` connections card** is marked INTENDED END-STATE now, but still draws a live Connect CTA for a provider `providers.ts` refuses. Either a truth/intended variant like `Connect.dc.html`, or mirror the registry.
  - **Store screenshots must not use "Holocene" / "Bon Iver"** — a real recording by a living artist paired with our own mood label is exactly what Spotify Developer Policy II.3 exists to prevent. Fictitious strings in every listing asset.
  - where: `scratchpad/canvas/*.dc.html`, `docs/SCREENS.md` DoD · done when: each bullet is closed or explicitly dropped with a reason · src: six `compliance-auditor` passes, 2026-08-26 · added: 2026-08-27
- [ ] `MB-009` `P2` **`markSeen()` is one-way and survives account deletion, so a second user on the same device never sees onboarding.** Independent of any legal question — it is a plain product defect.
  - `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/onboarding/onboardingStore.ts:8-14` documents the behaviour as deliberate: *"ONE-WAY. Once true it stays true forever; there is deliberately NO unset/reset."* It persists per **device**, not per account, and it is not cleared by logout **or by account delete**.
  - `routeMachine.ts:17` confirms onboarding is *"reachable from EXACTLY one state — a first-run device"*. Combined with the persistent Skip at `OnboardingScreen.tsx:126-134`, which calls `complete()` from panel 1, the entire onboarding is one tap from being permanently unreachable for every future user of that handset.
  - **Who this hits:** a shared or family device; a resold or refurbished phone; a user who deletes their account and starts again; anyone who taps Skip once. They land in the app with no introduction to what it does.
  - **Why it is more than cosmetic:** onboarding is where `UI-005` places the AI-disclosure line, and it is the only surface that explains the body reading before the consent gate. A screen that can be permanently dismissed by a previous owner of the phone is not a reliable place to put anything — which is why `UI-005` moved the notice into the persistent bottom chrome rather than onto a panel.
  - fix direction: key the flag per account rather than per device, or clear it on account delete and on logout. Either is a small change; the decision is which semantics are wanted, so it wants `architect` before `developer`.
  - where: `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/onboarding/onboardingStore.ts:8-14` · `OnboardingScreen.tsx:126-134` · `navigation/routeMachine.ts:17` · done when: a fresh account on a device that has already completed onboarding sees onboarding · src: `compliance-auditor` ruling on `UI-005`, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
  - **update 2026-09-24:** the approved plan's default is per-account onboarding (`docs/plans/DESIGN_V2_PLAN.md`, "defaults not asked"). H1 put the AI notice in the Onboarding chrome (`367a44a`, not merged); per-device onboarding still means Generate is the reliable first exposure.
- [ ] `UI-006` `P1` **The canvas pulls Plus Jakarta Sans and Space Mono from Google Fonts while `tokens.ts` declares system faces. Same class as the display-face defect, pointing the other way.**
  - `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/design/tokens.ts:243-247` declares `display: 'GeneralSans-Semibold'`, **`text: 'System'`**, **`mono: 'monospace'`**. All 23 boards' `<helmet>` links `fonts.googleapis.com` for Manrope, **Plus Jakarta Sans** and **Space Mono**, and `tokens.css` sets `--text` and `--mono` to them.
  - **So every board has always rendered body copy and mono labels in faces the app will never use.** The display-face defect was the canvas showing *nothing* where the app shows General Sans; this is the canvas showing *something specific* where the app shows whatever the OS provides. Both are the canvas depicting type the product cannot produce.
  - **The review files are already correct here and should not be changed** — `docs/review/*.html` declare `ui-sans-serif, -apple-system, "Segoe UI"` for `--text` and `ui-monospace, Consolas` for `--mono`, which *is* the system stack `tokens.ts` asks for. This was found while fixing the display face in those same files.
  - **Second-order:** it also makes every board render network-dependent. `reviewcheck.py:74-77` fails any external reference on a review file; `board.py` has no equivalent check on the canvas, so an offline or CI run silently measures a board typeset in Segoe UI and reports clean.
  - **The decision is not obvious and belongs to `designer`.** Either the canvas drops to system faces and matches the app (losing typographic control of every board), or `tokens.ts` gains real bundled text/mono faces and the app matches the canvas (a bundle-size and licensing question). Do not resolve it by editing one side.
  - where: all 23 `docs/canvas/*.dc.html` line 10 · `docs/canvas/tokens.css` · `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/design/tokens.ts:243-247` · done when: the canvas and the app declare the same text and mono faces, and `board.py` fails an external font reference · src: font-fix adversarial verification, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
  - **update 2026-09-30:** once PR #200 (HX-8) merges, the app's display face is `'System'`, while these still describe General Sans as the app's face: `docs/review/01-splash.html:120`, `02-onboarding.html` and `03-signin.html` ("The real screen uses General Sans…"); `docs/canvas/Tokens.dc.html:66-70` ("General Sans 600" cells); and `docs/canvas/tokens.css`'s `--disp`, which names "General Sans" first. The `designer`'s to align with F6 and Bellefair, unless plan step S8's canvas deletion removes them first (`ARCH-007`) (pr-hx8.md Follow-ups).
- [ ] `UI-005` `P0` **First-exposure disclosure that an AI system infers emotion. RULED TWICE by `compliance-auditor`; this is the second, buildable spec. Ships to SHIPPED FILES, not canvases.**
  - **Why it does not wait on `DAN-010`.** AI Act Art. 50(3)/(5) attach independently of the high-risk classification — Recital 132 ("irrespective of whether they qualify as high-risk or not"), Art. 50(6), Commission Service Desk. Art. 50 applied from **2 August 2026** and was NOT moved by the Digital Omnibus, which deferred only Annex III to 2 December 2027. Art. 50(1) attaches with **no biometric predicate at all**, so it survives every branch of the pivot.
  - **update 2026-09-29:** surfaces 1–3 merged to `main` in `c3beeb4` (PR #192). The ConsentSheet, the receipt and the next-launch cohort notice remain, so the row stays open.

  - **THE STATE-SWITCH FROM THE FIRST SPEC IS DEAD. Do not rebuild it.** It failed on five axes, three permanently:
    - `degraded` is set server-side (`anomalyFilter.js:380`, `affectEngine.js:623,686`) and **is emitted to no client**. `playlist_ready` carries `trigger, mode, reqId, params, tracks, familiar, discovery` (`biometricHandler.js:1288-1296`); the pulse DTO is `{domain, band, confidence, computedAt}` (`pulseController.js:129-134`). The app cannot know it is degraded.
    - **Baseline sufficiency** is unknowable client-side — the contract is `<10 samples → null (never fabricate)`, so "the baseline it has learned for you" is false for every new user.
    - `BIO_CONTEXT_PROMPT` (`biometricHandler.js:541`) is a **server env var the client cannot see**, and it gates whether the stored-profile snapshot runs at all.
    - **Consent granted ≠ OS permission granted** (`Consent.dc.html:92` says so itself).
    - **`connectStore.moodOnly` is one-way in BOTH wrong directions** (`connectStore.ts:29-56`, "there is no unresolve surface"): pair a watch after choosing mood-only and it stays `true` forever; withdraw consent after connecting and it stays `false` forever. It is the only flag named for the job and it is wrong for exactly the people who changed their mind.
    - **The root cause: I added an input list Art. 50 never asked for.** Commission FAQ, verbatim: the duty "does not entail providing information regarding e.g. the purpose of the system's operation" — it is bare notification of *operation*. That list was the sole source of a runtime problem the app cannot solve.

  - **THE WORDING — one invariant sentence, no runtime switch, true in every state:**
    > **An AI system infers your emotional state here. It is a machine's reading, not a person's — and it can be wrong.**
    - **"AI system", not "automated system".** Art. 50(1) requires people be informed they are interacting with *an AI system*; the "obvious" exemption is "to be construed narrowly". "Automated system" is GDPR Art. 22 register and reads as a rule-based script. One word, removes the argument. **It concedes nothing to `DAN-010`** — Art. 3(1) 'AI system' is met on any reading; the contested question is Art. 3(34) biometric data.
    - Nothing is softened. The claim is identical; only the unverifiable input list moved.
  - **CONSENT SCREEN — scope-framed, because a consent notice describes what *would* happen. HALT-grade defect in the first spec: assigning present-tense health-ON wording to a screen rendered BEFORE the user agrees is false for every user who declines, inside a GDPR Art. 9 notice, and it contradicts `Consent.dc.html:60` ("Until you decide, health reading is paused and mood-only keeps working") 40px away on the stale branch.**
    > **An AI system infers your emotional state. Today it does that from the point you place and what you tell it. If you agree here, it will also use your heart rate, your heart-rate variability, your sleep, and the baseline it builds from them. It is a machine's reading, not a person's — and it can be wrong.**
    - Carries strictly MORE than the old health-ON variant, and is false in no state.
  - **MIX RECEIPT — where the input enumeration becomes true by construction.** Server-authored per generation (`NowPlayingScreen.tsx:207`). The server knows `degraded`, knows whether a baseline exists, knows whether the snapshot ran. **The client never will.** Emit the evidence set with the receipt and render it — this is the only place "the baseline it has learned for you" can be honest, and it gives a truthful degraded surface for free.

  - **THE PLACEMENT — SHIPPED FILES. The first spec named three design canvases; an Art. 50 notice that exists only on a canvas informs nobody.**
    1. **`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/experience/generate/GenerateScreen.tsx`** — invariant sentence, persistent, above the fold, between hero and the Live/Manual toggle. `content.secondary` and body size are the MINIMUM, and it must sit **on `textScrimFill`** (`GenerateScreen.tsx:248,353`) or it will not hold AA over the moving Living Aurora. Not `pointerEvents="none"`. **There is no verdict block in the shipped screen** — that is `Main.dc.html:96-100` only. Re-anchor when it ships; do not wait for it.
    2. **`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/onboarding/OnboardingScreen.tsx`** — in the **persistent bottom chrome** (`:137`, beside the pager dots), visible on all three panels. **NOT on a panel:** there is no "Settled, fairly even" panel in the shipped app (copy is `['Feel it.','Your body is heard.','Your soundtrack, tuned to you.']`, `:39`), Skip calls `complete()` from panel 1 (`:126-134`), and `markSeen()` is one-way and survives logout AND account-delete (`onboardingStore.ts:8-14`).
    3. **`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/experience/pulse/PulseScreen.tsx`** — adjacent to the state headline. **ADDED; both earlier specs omitted it.** This is the shipped surface that actually displays an inferred state — `friendlyStatus()` passes through labels like "Deep Focus / Flow State" (`statusLabels.ts:20-24`). By the spec's own reasoning it is a stronger Art. 50(3) surface than the unbuilt onboarding panel, and it is where `C5(b)` lands anyway.
    4. **`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/experience/profile/ConsentSheet.tsx`** (mirror on `docs/canvas/Consent.dc.html`) — scope-framed wording, distinct band, **in the FIXED chrome above the ScrollView at `:199`** (anything scrollable can be scrolled out of view — the footer failure in a different hat), above the stale banner, with a real boundary rather than whitespace, outside the mono-label grid vocabulary, not gated behind Agree.
    5. **`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/experience/playback/NowPlayingScreen.tsx`** + a server evidence set — the per-read enumeration.
    6. **Next-launch notice for the already-onboarded cohort** — moot today (≤5 hand-enrolled users), required the moment the cohort is not hand-enrolled.
    - **Delete `Consent.dc.html:94`'s buried line.** Note it exists only on the canvas — **the shipped app makes no automated-processing statement at all today.**
    - Not needed: Splash, Login. Install is not exposure.

  - **GATES.**
    - **`C5(b)` gates this. `C5(a)` does not.** My earlier rationale was **factually wrong**: the Play Health-apps-declaration trigger is *health-data access*, not on-screen copy, so this notice "converts C5 from arguable to unarguable" is false — **C5 has been unarguable since Health Connect was wired.** Split it: **C5(a)** is the "not a medical device" line in the app-store description — a Play Console action on a listing that does not exist yet, and it stays a pre-submission gate. **C5(b)** is the in-app echo on Pulse and the Receipt head — one string, same PR class, same surfaces as this notice. Gating an EU-law duty behind a listing edit for an unlaunched app is a worse exposure than shipping the notice alone.
    - **Contrast:** `textScrimFill` treatment, like every other text cluster on the Generate screen. `--ink2` at body size is necessary and NOT sufficient over the aurora.
    - **Accessibility:** single readable text node, correct reading order, in the accessibility tree, never `aria-hidden`. **Art. 50(5) itself creates no accessibility standard** — the Guidelines say so explicitly. The candidate regime is the **European Accessibility Act (Dir. (EU) 2019/882)** via **EN 301 549 / WCAG 2.1 AA**, with a **microenterprise exemption (Art. 4(5))** as a live counter-case. Practically it does not gate us — `docs/SCREENS.md:195` already commits to WCAG 2.2 AA app-wide. Record the regime; do not record it as settled.
    - **UNVERIFIED, correctly left open:** whether an on-screen emotional-inference claim changes the declared Apple App Privacy categories. Apple's categories describe data *collected* and this notice changes nothing about collection — a reason to think it is fine, not a citation.
    - **Sequencing question routed to `regulatory-researcher` with `DAN-010`, NOT settled here:** Art. 3(4) excludes use "in the course of a personal non-professional activity" from the *deployer* definition, so whether a pre-launch app with ≤5 developer-enrolled users engages the Art. 50(3) deployer duty **today** is genuinely open. **Not a reason to delay** — the duty is certain at launch and the work is identical — but the "26 days late" framing should not be used to pull `C5(a)` forward on a false urgency.
  - **Do not soften the wording to reduce exposure.** Nothing here settles `DAN-010`, and a copy edit is not a defence — Art. 3(39) is an objective purpose test.
  - where: the six shipped files above · done when: all carry the correct wording, it renders above the fold in both faces with an AA-holding scrim, `C5(b)` has shipped, and `compliance-auditor` has cleared the BUILT result · src: `compliance-auditor` rulings 2026-08-28 (×2) · added: 2026-08-28
  - **update 2026-09-24:** H1 (branch `fix/h1-ai-disclosure-interim`, nine commits ending `1539029`) implements surfaces 1–3 — Generate, the Onboarding chrome and Pulse — plus `C5(b)`'s in-app "not a medical device" line on Pulse. Fix built on that branch, awaiting review and merge — close those surfaces on merge evidence. It is not device-proven (`DAN-017`), and still owes regulatory G5 on the copy, a designer SHIP, resilience G10 and compliance on the built result. Reviewers must also settle two brief conflicts the builder resolved: the Pulse band sits after the StateCard rather than inside it (compliance to rule whether that is "adjacent to the headline"), and the long sentence on Generate with the short one on Onboarding and Pulse (designer to confirm). **Still open after H1 merges: the ConsentSheet sentence (waits for counsel's copy and the 1→3 version bump, plan P5), the Now Playing receipt enumeration (plan P4), and the next-launch notice for the already-onboarded cohort (counsel, `DAN-014`).** The "Pre-Panic" half is `UI-007`.
  - **update 2026-09-26:** surfaces 1–3 (Generate, the Onboarding chrome, Pulse), the Pulse half of `C5(b)` and Play's "consult a healthcare professional" reminder (`MEDICAL_CONSULT_REMINDER`, `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/legal/legalCopy.ts:24` on the branch): fix on PR #192 (`fix/h1-ai-disclosure-interim`, head `97d4ca5`), gates passed incl. device evidence (G12 on `73a266e`, the typing-state recheck on `97d4ca5`); close on merge. Still open after it: surface 4 (`UI-012`); surface 5, together with `C5(b)` on the Receipt head, which `docs/plans/HALT_CONSENT_VERSION_RETENTION_AND_DECLARATIONS.md:86` prescribes alongside Pulse; surface 6; `C5(a)` in the listing (`OPS-034`); the spoken TalkBack pass and 3-button captures (`UI-026`); the Generate typing follow-ups (`UI-025`). Two notes from `regulatory-researcher` on this row's reasoning, which stays as written above: the Art. 50 Guidelines (the examples after point 31) list AI-enabled recommender systems among systems that do not interact directly with people, so "Art. 50(1) … survives every branch" is not settled (counsel); and point 100 says the duty applies even when the system runs ex post, so surface 6 is not "moot" — users enrolled since 2 August 2026 have been processed by the nightly worker and the recompute with no notice (rated P0 for counsel's triage, `DAN-014`). Branch B — a short notice on the Connect wearable card, because a wearable user's first inference can run there before Generate renders — stays with counsel (`DAN-014`).

Both rules are the owner's (`docs/plans/DESIGN_V2_PLAN.md:40`). The numeric red-exclusion band that V1 now fixes (`.claude/commands/vision-frame.md:7`) is what measures whether they collide.

The owner ruled set B out on 2026-09-29 (`DEC-038`), and the designer's V0 synthesis (§2 T4) finds set A's intense-to-joy blend crossing the red band too, at about C 0.054, h 25°. ## Infra · CI · repo hygiene

- [ ] `OPS-020` `P2` **`regulatory-researcher` — a seventh build agent, created 2026-08-28. Standing routing, not a one-off.** Its first assignment is `DAN-010`.
  - **Why it exists.** Six agents covered code, design, resilience, hygiene and *provider terms*. None covered **law**. `compliance-auditor` reads a company's TOS and branding rules; when a finding turned out to rest on the EU AI Act rather than on anybody's terms of service, there was nobody to route it to and the analysis fell to me — which is exactly how a build-side agent ends up producing something that reads like legal advice.
  - **Scope:** EU technology regulation — AI Act, GDPR, and the adjacent instruments this product touches (ePrivacy, DSA, the MDR/wellness boundary, the Data Act). Read-only, plus web.
  - **THE BOUNDARY, and it is the point of the agent: it prepares the file, it does not decide.** Creating an agent does not move a legal classification from counsel to the build team. It never presents a conclusion as settled, never writes "we are compliant", and refuses a verdict on a question of law — it returns a branch map instead. **It is preparation for a lawyer, never a substitute for one.**
  - **Two rules written in from what went wrong here:** a reproduction site (`gdpr-info.eu`, `artificialintelligenceact.eu`) is adequate to RAISE a question and inadequate to SETTLE one, and is marked as needing OJ confirmation — the entire current AI Act analysis rests on one. And **unretrieved is a finding, not a gap to paper over**: name the document, why it failed, what it would settle, how a human gets it. Never substitute a secondary summary and then reason as though the primary had been read.
  - **Output format:** IN FORCE NOW / APPLIES FROM · THE PIVOT · OBLIGATIONS THAT ATTACH REGARDLESS · BRANCH MAP · THE COUNTER-CASE · UNRETRIEVED · QUESTIONS FOR COUNSEL · WHAT I AM NOT SAYING. The *attach-regardless* section is deliberately first-class: it is the only part the build may act on before counsel replies.
  - where: `.claude/agents/regulatory-researcher.md` · rostered in `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/ORCHESTRATOR_FABLE.md` · done when: nothing to build — this row exists so the agent is discoverable and its boundary is not re-litigated. Close it once `DAN-010` has counsel's answer. · src: Daniel, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28

- [ ] `OPS-001` `P1` **OWNED BY A SEPARATE SESSION — see the block below before acting.** The Wave-4 mission loop is not running. ~~`docs/plans/WAVE4_HALT` is present (2026-08-25 16:05) and no session has run since.~~ **The halt file is now ABSENT — see the finding below; the loop is stopped because no process is running it, not because of the flag.** `W4-015` (soak, closeout & package) is the **only remaining MUST** and has never seriously started; the 24 h `RUN_SOAK=1` run has never executed. `#verify`
  - ### 🔒 OWNED BY ANOTHER SESSION — DO NOT TOUCH, DO NOT RESTART, DO NOT ARCHIVE
    **Recorded 2026-09-02 by Daniel.** A **separate session owns the Wave-4 mission loop** and is pushing its work to git. This row exists so nobody picks it up twice — that is its whole purpose now. Concretely, for any session that is not that one:
    - **Do not run `scripts\run-mission.ps1`.** The runner takes a machine-wide lock and refuses a second instance, but the failure mode of trying is a confusing half-start, not a clean refusal.
    - **Do not archive `WAVE4_STATE.md` or `WAVE4_ARCHIVE.md`** (328 KB and 460 KB, both past the mission's own 150 KB threshold). The archival debt is real and it is **not yours** — it belongs to the owning session, which is the only one that can do it without racing a live writer.
    - **Do not create, delete or 'clean up' `docs/plans/WAVE4_HALT`.** It is the loop's stop switch.

  - ### ⚠️ FINDING — the halt file vanished and I cannot attribute it
    Reported honestly rather than quietly: **`docs/plans/WAVE4_HALT` existed at 2026-08-25 19:05 (0 bytes) when this session listed it during a status pass, and is now absent from everywhere on disk** — including `.worktree-surface-removal`. It is not tracked in `HEAD` and not on `origin/main`; historically it has been committed and cleared (`8d3e53e` halt, `0e5ae8f` clear), so the copy seen was an untracked local flag.
    - **I cannot attribute the removal.** The obvious suspect is this session's own merge → abort → stash-pop cycle against `origin/main`, and I am not going to claim otherwise; a second session is also live on this tree and the loop's own reflection #15 recorded the file **ABSENT** at its entry, so the timeline is genuinely ambiguous.
    - **What it changes:** `OPS-001`'s stated reason for the halt — *"`WAVE4_HALT` is present"* — no longer holds. **But the flag's absence does not start anything.** Verified 2026-09-02: no mission lockfile exists and no loop process is running. The loop is stopped because nothing is running it, not because of the flag. Whoever owns it should treat the stop switch as **unset** and re-set it deliberately if they want it held.

- [ ] `OPS-008` `P2` ~~`mobile-ios-build` has never gone green.~~ **MODULAR-HEADERS HALF CLOSED ON EVIDENCE 2026-08-26 — iOS now builds.** Fixed by two targeted lines in `mobile/KokonadaHealth/ios/Podfile:35-36` (`pod 'GoogleUtilities', :modular_headers => true` / `pod 'RecaptchaInterop', :modular_headers => true`), deliberately targeted rather than a global `use_modular_headers!` which would change how all ~70 RN pods build. **Proof, checked per `OPS-010` rather than trusting the badge:** run `32992549921`, `event=push`, sha `0e19ad8` on `main` — `Run ruby/setup-ruby@v1 -> success`, `CocoaPods install -> success`, **`Xcode build (simulator, unsigned) -> success`**. Every step executed; nothing was filtered out. **Still open:** the second half of this row's own DoD — no `mobile/KokonadaHealth/Gemfile.lock` is committed, so CocoaPods re-resolves from scratch on every run and a future upstream release can break it again with no diff to point at. → `DAN-006`
  - where: `mobile/KokonadaHealth/ios/Podfile` (no `use_modular_headers!` anywhere in it) · job `mobile-ios-build` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` · done when: one green `mobile-ios-build` run that reaches **and passes** `xcodebuild`, with `mobile/KokonadaHealth/Gemfile.lock` committed from that same session · src: CI runs `32974962408` and `32975663708` on PR #180, 2026-08-26 · added: 2026-08-26
  - **Not caused by the dependency cleanup, and this was checked rather than assumed.** `da9e532` never touched the Podfile; the Podfile is byte-identical between `origin/main` and the branch; `@react-native-google-signin/google-signin` is `^16.1.2` on both. **`pod install` fails the same way on `main` today.** The job is simply the first thing that has ever run `pod install` in this repo's CI, and it surfaced a pre-existing defect that jest, the Android job and a Metro bundle check are all structurally blind to.
  - two candidate remedies, both named by CocoaPods in the error itself: `use_modular_headers!` globally in the Podfile, or `:modular_headers => true` on `GoogleUtilities` and `RecaptchaInterop` specifically. Prefer the narrow one if it works — the global switch changes how every pod in the project is built.
  - **`Gemfile.lock` is generated in the SAME session, not before it.** It is absent today, so bundler re-resolves on every run and two iOS runs are not comparable. A lockfile produced from a guessed `bundle install` pins a state nobody proved; one produced in the session where `pod install` succeeds **and** the app compiles pins a state that is known good. Both layers lock together or neither does.
  - **do not make `mobile-ios-build` a required check until this row closes** — it cannot pass, and until the lockfile exists its results are not reproducible run to run.
  - what only a Mac can settle: whether the app still **compiles for iOS** with `lottie-react-native` and `react-native-vector-icons` removed. Both were autolinked with codegen, so their removal touched the iOS native surface, and nothing in CI or on a Windows box can answer that. It is the question this job exists for and it is still unanswered.

Lands as part of `BE-009` — see that row.

- [ ] `OPS-011` `P0` **A PR into any branch other than `main` gets no CI whatsoever, so every stacked PR this project has merged was merged unverified.** `on.pull_request.branches: [main]` fires only for main-targeted PRs. Verified: PR #182 (`fix/ios-pods-modular-headers` → `feat/intelligence-wave`) has **zero** Actions runs.
  - **DO NOT CITE A STACKED PR'S GREEN CHECKS AS EVIDENCE.** PR #181 displays green only because it was *created* against `main` and retargeted to `feat/intelligence-wave` afterwards. A `pull_request` run tests the **merge of head into base**, and at run time that base was `main` — so #181's checks are evidence for `merge(3b3a427, main)`, **a tree that will never exist**. Merging #181 creates `merge(3b3a427, feat/intelligence-wave)`, which nothing has ever tested, and the wave branch has moved (`b821d3b` → `cc5a23c`) since that run. The head sha is unchanged, which is exactly why the checks look trustworthy and are not.
  - This is the second independent way a green check means nothing in this repo — see `OPS-010` for the first (paths-filtered native jobs reporting success without executing a step). Together they mean "CI is green" is not a statement about the code unless you have checked *which jobs ran* and *which merge they ran against*.

So the dark pair has **zero** production consumers. Their only remaining reference is the AA sweep in `src/design/__tests__/tokens.test.ts:47-49`, which iterates both faces and therefore now proves AA for a pairing the app never renders — the kind of test that keeps a dead token alive indefinitely.

- [ ] `OPS-016` `P2` **The mobile suite fails 6 suites / 60 tests on a COLD cache under full parallel load, and passes 93/93 when those same six run alone.** Proven 2026-08-28: `jest --clearCache` then full run → `OfflineBanner`, `shadow.experience`, `GenerateScreen`, `ConnectServicesWearable`, `HistoryScreen`, `UpNextSheet` red; the identical six `--runInBand` cold → **all pass, 18.4s**. So it is worker contention while babel compiles, not masked real failures — but it means **a cold CI run is currently a coin flip**, and it has already absorbed two `jest.setTimeout(20000)` bumps as symptom relief.
  - **Why it matters beyond noise:** a suite that is red for timing reasons cannot distinguish "we broke something" from "the machine was busy", which is exactly the false-signal class this project keeps getting caught by. It also makes every future auditor pass argue about the baseline instead of the change.
  - candidate fixes, in order of honesty: raise `maxWorkers` headroom or pin `--runInBand` for CI cold runs · warm the babel cache as a CI step before the suite · or a per-file `jest.setTimeout` only where compile cost is genuinely the cause (already done for `EmptyState`, `VaultConsentPanel`, and six pre-existing siblings).
  - where: `mobile/KokonadaHealth/jest.config.js` · `.github/workflows/ci.yml` · done when: three consecutive cold full runs are green · src: proven while closing the `resilience-auditor` re-audit of `feat/primary-cta-ink-fill`, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
  - **update 2026-09-26 — new measurements from Track H:** a cold cache fails 5 suites / 50 tests on main and 4 / 34 on PR #192; under load the same suites time out on their first test at 5 000 ms — `ConnectServicesWearable` (six more then fail with "Can't access .root on unmounted test renderer"), `shadow.experience`'s ATTACK 6 (it `require`s GenerateScreen inside the test body; it hit the limit once on a cold run), `UpNextSheet.render`, `OfflineBanner`, `WhyAccordion`, and `HistoryScreen` at times. None of the first three sets `jest.setTimeout` (checked `src/__tests__/shadow.experience.test.tsx:177-183`, `src/experience/connect/__tests__/ConnectServicesWearable.test.tsx`, `src/experience/playback/__tests__/UpNextSheet.render.test.tsx`), and `jest.config.js` has no `testTimeout`. The reviewers' class fix: a global `testTimeout` for render suites or pre-requiring React Native's internals in setup, the cold run as the gate, and mounted trees unmounted in `afterEach`.
  - **update 2026-09-30 — the Track HX runs:** whichever suite that keeps jest's default 5000 ms runs first in a worker pays the cold Babel and React Native import inside its first test, and times out under load; the losers were `shadow.experience` (ATTACK 6), `UpNextSheet`, `UpNextSheet.render`, `ConnectServicesWearable` and, once, `SystemStateDock`. Untouched main failed the same way in 25 of 42 isolated runs, and the saved full runs passed at `--maxWorkers=3`. The fix proposed: `jest.setTimeout(20000)` in `shadow.experience`, `UpNextSheet`, `UpNextSheet.render` and `ConnectServicesWearable`, as `PulseScreen.test.tsx` already carries (HANDOFF §9; pr-hx8.md and pr-hx7.md Follow-ups; pr-hx2.md, pr-hx4.md and pr-hx6.md).

    - `UpNextSheet.test.tsx`: under parallel load, one first-test timeout was followed by 24 more failures in the same file, 19 of them "Can't access .root on unmounted test renderer", with "overlapping act() calls" warnings and assertions whose mock was never called, so one flake shows as a suite-wide red; the mechanism is unproven, perhaps an `act()` the timed-out test leaves open (hx4 developer round 3). Done when also: the heavy render suites get per-suite timeouts, or settle and unmount in `afterEach`, and a run under load passes.
    - `PulseScreen.test.tsx`: each test unmounts its tree at the end of its own body, so a failed assertion leaves the screen mounted; `afterEach` then resets `warmStore` and `pulseStateStore` outside `act` (`:88-90`), and the orphaned screen's subscriptions (`PulseScreen.tsx:230-239`) logged 48 "not wrapped in act(...)" warnings that buried the one real failure (hx7 developer round 3, LOW). Done when also: trees are tracked and unmounted in `afterEach` before the store resets, and a deliberately failing test leaves no warnings in later tests. The lines re-checked by the scribe on `fd68094`.

Done when also: the repo's testing notes say to compare testIDs or booleans, never instances.
- [ ] `OPS-017` `P3` **`SpotifyAttribution.test.tsx:56,59` is inert only by coincidence.** It is the one of nine `jest.spyOn(RN, 'useColorScheme')` holdouts that renders without forcing a face. `spyOn` cannot install on the preset's already-mocked `useColorScheme` (`jest-mock/build/index.js:737`), so `mockReturnValue` permanently mutates the shared mock and the `jest.restoreAllMocks()` at `:43` is dead code reading as protection it does not give. It currently survives because `:59` happens to set `'light'`, which equals the preset default. **Reorder the file or add a face-sensitive assertion after `:65` and it silently misreads.** The other eight holdouts force the face on every render and are inert by construction.
  - fix: convert this file alone to capture-and-restore (`forceFace`/`releaseFace`, as `4f78938` did), or at minimum comment the no-op so the dead `restoreAllMocks` is not mistaken for a guard · src: `resilience-auditor` re-audit, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28
- [ ] `OPS-019` `P1` **Build a structured HTTP access logger — mounted BEFORE `apiLimiter`.** There is none today: no `morgan`/`pino`/`winston` in `package.json`, no logging middleware anywhere in `index.js:40-103`, and nothing in `app/middleware/` logs at all. Every log line in production is application-level (`[healthBatch]`, `[reclassify]`, `[gen.targets]`), so there is no record that a request ever arrived.
  - **The ordering is the whole requirement.** `apiLimiter` sits at `index.js:94` ahead of every router and answers 429 without calling `next()`, so anything mounted *after* it cannot see a rate-limited request. A logger placed after the limiter would reproduce exactly the blindness that makes `BE-014` unanswerable. It must sit ahead of `apiLimiter` and must record the rate-limit outcome.
  - **This is a documented requirement that was never built, not a new idea.** `docs/ORCHESTRATOR_FABLE.md` `<engineering_excellence>` already requires *"Sentry … + structured JSON logs with correlation ids"* and *"No silent failures — every catch path emits a metric, never a swallowed error."*
  - **Sequencing, and it is deliberate:** this lands BEFORE any decision about giving an agent Railway log access. Granting a broad Railway token today would only permit tailing silence — the logs contain no request lines to read. Build the instrument, then decide the credential. (Options weighed: Railway CLI + `RAILWAY_TOKEN` — broad, can read variables and trigger deploys, not read-only; a log drain to a queryable store — scoped credential, some setup; a per-incident exported file — zero standing credential but keeps Daniel as courier.)
  - minimum fields: method, path, status, duration, a correlation id, and whether the limiter rejected. **No vitals, no tokens** — `<locked_decisions>` zero-knowledge biometrics and the W4-D03/D10 precedent both apply.
  - where: `backend/app/index.js` above `:94` · done when: a 429 appears in the logs with its path · src: 429 investigation, 2026-08-28 · added: 2026-08-28

A deliberate build identifier removes the luck.

  - Same family as `OPS-017` (a `spyOn` on a preset mock).
  - **update 2026-10-01 — two more, and a trap (the HX-3 mobile developer's build report, NEW-FINDINGs P3 and INFO):** (1) `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/auth/__tests__/GoogleSignInButton.test.tsx:85`'s `jest.spyOn(RN, 'useWindowDimensions')` reaches no component: react-native's index is CommonJS without `__esModule`, so `import * as RN` is babel's copy and the spy patches only the copy. The "identical at 1.0 and 2.0" claim is checked only through the `PixelRatio` spy; nothing is hidden yet, because the button does not read `useWindowDimensions`. The idiom that reaches the hook is `jest.spyOn(RN.Dimensions, 'get')`, as `src/experience/connect/__tests__/ProviderRow.test.tsx:57` does on `fix/hx3-youtube`. (2) `src/experience/profile/__tests__/ProfileScreen.test.tsx`'s `beforeEach` `jest.clearAllMocks()` (`:139-140`) does not drop unconsumed `mock*Once` queues, so a test that fails before using its queued answer hands it to the next test; a never-resolving one hangs that test for 20 s and fails every test after it (one real failure produced 34 during HX-3's RED). The HX-2 tests at `:677`, `:695`, `:710` and `:734` have that shape; HX-3's tests queue just before the press and clean up in `finally`. (3) The jest preset reports `fontScale` 2 by default (`node_modules/@react-native/jest-preset/jest/mocks/NativeModules.js:41,47`), so a screen suite that sets no scale renders the large-text layout (Pulse in one column, the stacked Connect and Profile rows); a test that asserts layout must set the scale itself. Done when also: (1) spies on `RN.Dimensions.get`; and (2) resets the mocks and re-primes them in `beforeEach`, or queues each once-answer just before its use. The lines re-checked by the scribe on `bbae791`.

- [ ] `OPS-036` `P2` **Nothing machine-checks the design-v2 gate record yet: `/page` G13 and `/merge` confirm by hand that `docs/design-v2/pages/<page>/gates.json` holds a passing, non-stale verdict for every gate G0–G12.** The plan asks for a CI check on `gates.json` for every PR that touches v2 files (`docs/plans/DESIGN_V2_PLAN.md:230,295`). Under the architect's S5 design (fork B1, option B) a verdict is stale when `git diff --quiet <inputSha> HEAD -- <inputs>` fails, so the guard needs a full-history checkout (`fetch-depth: 0`), which only the gitleaks job has today (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:36`). With it: `gates.json` and `verdicts/` are never inputs, and page branches are never rebased or force-pushed.
  - where: `.claude/commands/page.md:13-16,35` · `.claude/commands/merge.md:5` · `.github/workflows/ci.yml` · done when: a CI job with a full-history checkout fails a v2 page PR whose `gates.json` lacks a passing, non-stale verdict for any of G0–G12, proven red on a deliberately stale verdict and green on a fresh one — built by `developer` with `resilience-auditor` before P1's G13 · src: architect S5 report (fork B1; order step 3), 2026-09-29 · added: 2026-09-29
  - **update 2026-09-29 (the S5 review fixes):** the rule quoted above is replaced. A verdict is now stale when `git -C <page worktree root> diff --quiet <inputSha> <target> -- <inputs>`, with `gates.json` and `verdicts/` excluded, exits non-zero. `<inputs>` is what the per-gate inputs rule prescribes today, never the field the entry recorded, and G13 counts a roster of required agents per gate (`.claude/commands/page.md:13-17,35`; `merge.md:5`). The CI check must also cover:
    - **The nine RED cases from `resilience-auditor`, each watched failing before the evaluator passes it, against a scratch git repo:**
      1. The bare input `charter.md` is rejected at record time.
      2. A token change merged from main stales G11 and G12.
      3. An N/A entry with empty inputs is rejected.
      4. G8 fails when one component is at REVISE and another at SHIP.
      5. A failing verdict without the SHA, followed by a passing re-request, leaves the failure recorded and counted.
      6. A G11 entry does not count when its manifest SHA differs from its inputSha.
      7. After a squash, on a fresh clone, a verdict reads stale without `git fetch origin pull/<n>/head` and non-stale with it.
      8. `merge-base --is-ancestor` blocks the second PR in a batch when it lacks current main.
      9. A verdict file modified after it was added voids the record.
      Pin one fixture whose recorded inputs are narrower than the rule prescribes. Without it, an evaluator that reads the recorded inputs still passes cases 1 and 2.
    - **It implements `/page`'s content rule, not the plan's wording.** `DESIGN_V2_PLAN.md:230` says, in Hebrew, that the check fails when the SHA does not match. An exact match can never hold, because every scribe commit moves HEAD.
    - **Until it is a required status check on `main`,** a merge from the GitHub UI skips G13, and "Update branch → Update with rebase" rewrites a page branch against `page.md:10`. Making it required is a GitHub settings step for Daniel.
    - **Two pieces the new text assumes:**
      - the guard test that keeps each page's `row.md` identical to the plan's table (`page.md:16`);
      - a pinned import-graph tool for G13's closure check. `page.md:16` runs `npx --yes madge` unpinned, as `custodian.md:41` does for its ad hoc tools.
    - src: `resilience-auditor` S5 review (report, closing evidence; note, MEDIUM), 2026-09-29
  - **update 2026-09-29:** L1 QA registry (`resilience-auditor`, §4 item 15): this check is `QA-074`, MISSING, and making it a required status check waits on the owner (`DEC-093`). Its sibling pipeline guards, all due before P1's G0 and G13, are `QA-075` (`OPS-056`), `QA-076` (`OPS-059`) and `QA-083` (`OPS-010`).

The full run on `bbae791` failed this way, on an id containing "77". - [ ] `OPS-093` `P3` **Three of HX-3's backend guards check less than they claim: the seal-override guard has two blind spots, the disconnect test's op recorder can miss a write, and the seam test's "no sentinel in ANY outbound HTTP request" spies only the axios verbs.** Each is a false-green risk in a test, not a production gap, and none hides a defect today.
  - (1) the seal-override guard (N5): it scans mocks only under `backend/tests`, keyed by file name (`backend/tests/youtubeSealOverride.structural.test.js:51-55`), while Jest's default `testMatch` also runs `backend/app/**/__tests__/*.test.js` (the backend's Jest config in `backend/package.json` sets no `testMatch` or `roots`) and `isTestPath` (`:41`) leaves those files out of the non-test scan, so a seal mock there is seen by neither rule; and `MOCK_CALL` (`:85`) misses a variable path such as `jest.mock(SEAL_PATH, …)`, because `\bseal\b` does not match `SEAL_PATH`. Jest mocks never leak between files, so the risk is a false green in that file. The `resilience-auditor` classified the DT-B3 seal mock a fixture on condition that this is closed (`DEC-123`).
  - (2) the disconnect op recorder (N6), `backend/tests/youtubeDisconnect.integration.test.js:140-162`: the window closes at the response (`:144-146`), so a deferred `setImmediate` write can land after both the recorder and the `after` snapshot; raw-driver writes through `mongoose.connection.db.collection` bypass mongoose's debug hook; only the `users` and `musicprofiles` documents are snapshotted (`:135-136`); and the network spy covers only axios's `get`, `post`, `put`, `delete` and `request` (`:99-101`). T3 itself is confirmed by reading the handler (`backend/app/controllers/integrationsController.js:428-441`: one read, then return).
  - (3) the seam test (N7): it claims "no sentinel in ANY outbound HTTP request" (`backend/tests/youtubeEgressSeam.integration.test.js:24`) but spies only axios's verb methods (`:175-188`). The `resilience-auditor` found no `axios(` call, no `axios.create`, no `axios.patch` or `axios.head` and no global `fetch` in `backend/app`, so the claim holds today.
  - where: the three files above · done when: (1) a planted seal mock in a `backend/app/**/__tests__` file and one through a variable path each fail the guard; (2) the recorder stays on for about 100 ms after the response before `after` is taken, and every collection's document count and hash is compared; (3) a structural pin forbids the other HTTP forms in `backend/app`, or the claim is narrowed in the test and in the PR body · related: `BE-022`, `BE-035`, `DEC-122`, `DEC-123`, `OPS-031` · src: `resilience-auditor`'s HX-3 round 1 at `fc62215` (N5, N6 and N7, LOW; §4 item 2, the DT-B3 condition), 2026-10-01; the lines re-checked by the scribe on `fc62215` · added: 2026-10-01

## Architecture & surface removals

- [ ] `ARCH-001` `P1` Delete `watch/` — the abandoned Garmin Connect IQ app. Verified as a true leaf: no path reference from backend or mobile, nothing in CI, docker or any `package.json`. `#verify`
  - **THE TRAP:** the backend `/watch/*` lane is **live** and is the app's only real-time HR ingress — its client is the phone (`bleHeartRate.ts`, `liveHrClient.ts`, `restFallback.ts`, started on every app boot via `appBootstrap.ts:33`). Only the pairing-code flow is dead: `/watch/pair`, `/watch/pair/exchange`, `WatchPairingCard`, the pairing half of `watchPairingClient.ts`. One careless grep on "watch" or "garmin" takes out a live lane.
  - `watchPairingStore.ts:26` shows the dead card reaching into the live credential path — the revoke must be **re-homed, not dropped**.
  - preserve the provenance comment at `hrMeasurement.ts:11` (`HR_DELTA_BPM = 8`, `LIVENESS_MS = 45000` were mirrored from the sideloaded watch app). Keep no `garmin/` subfolder — that recreates the surface being deleted.
  - needs `architect` + an ADR (it amends the locked surface split in `ORCHESTRATOR_FABLE.md` `<standards>`) + `compliance-auditor`.
- [x] `ARCH-002` `P1` Delete `frontend/` — the web app, no users, actively costing money. Nothing needs to be served to a browser. `#verify` `#blocked` → `BE-002`, `DAN-002`, `DAN-003`
  - **CLOSED ON EVIDENCE 2026-09-23 — `frontend/` was deleted in PR #187** (180 files), together with the dead watch pairing routes and the browser-only YouTube endpoints. What remains is outside the repo: the two Vercel projects and the OAuth console entries — that is `DAN-002`.
  - deleting the web does **not** break Spotify or YouTube OAuth returns — the deep-link branch already ships and Spotify uses it end to end. Only `BE-001`'s `'web'` fallback and `BE-003`'s Garmin sites need work.
  - needs `architect` + an ADR + `compliance-auditor`; `BE-002` is the real cost of this task.
  - **DO NOT invert `middleware/csrf.js` to deny-all-origins.** Settled 2026-09-01 under `DAN-008`: React Native sends no `Origin` header (proven against RN 0.86.0's own `Libraries/Network/` source), so the inversion would 403 every request the real client makes. It is also unnecessary — PR #184 retired the cookie auth plane, so there is no ambient credential left for a cross-site forgery to ride on. The guard is now defence-in-depth against a threat model that no longer exists; when `frontend/` goes, **delete the guard rather than inverting it**, and say why in the ADR.
  - `/watch/pair` and `/watch/pair/exchange` are removed **here**, not with the watch app — their last consumer is `frontend/src/lib/api.ts:153` and it dies in this branch.
- [ ] `ARCH-003` `P1` Stand up a minimal landing page — the smallest thing that satisfies the store requirements. One static site, no framework, no build step, no design system, no dependency added anywhere; placeholder content, marked as such. **Do not salvage it out of `frontend/`** — a page carved from the app being deleted drags the build, the deps and the drift back in, and then the removal was theatre.
  - **UNBLOCKED 2026-08-27 — no domain purchase is needed for this. GitHub Pages satisfies the store URLs.** Verified against current terms: Play's account-deletion-URL page states the requirements in full as *"The weblink must be functional (for example, loads without error), relevant in scope (for example, the pathway to request account deletion should be prominently featured and easily discoverable on the page) and reference the app or developer name (that is, as it appears on your store listing in Google Play)."* — [support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13327111](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13327111). **There is no domain-ownership or verification requirement anywhere in it**, and no restriction on the host. Same for the privacy-policy URL, whose stated conditions are active, publicly accessible, non-geofenced, not a PDF, and non-editable. A `*.github.io` page meets all of these, and this repo already lives on GitHub.
  - **NOTE THE THIRD CONDITION — it is easy to miss and it is a real constraint:** the page must *reference the app or developer name as it appears on the store listing*. A generic placeholder does not satisfy it.
  - **NEVER a fabricated or placeholder-only URL.** Google checks reachability, and a dead or empty page is grounds for rejection — but more importantly this URL is a declaration to users about their own data, biometrics included. The page must genuinely exist and genuinely explain deletion.
  - **minimum content, from the cited terms:** a real home page that is not a login page and not a redirect; a visible link from it to the privacy policy; a **privacy policy** covering developer identity + contact, every data type collected, the sub-processor list (Groq, MongoDB Atlas, Railway), "never for advertising, never sold", the retention windows from `PRIVACY_DECLARATIONS.md`, how to revoke consent and request deletion, GDPR Art.9 basis and Art.15/17/20 rights; a **dedicated account-deletion page** at its own URL, prominently discoverable, usable by someone who has lost app access; and a **support page** with real contact details (Apple requires legal address, email, phone as local law demands).
  - Apple needs deletion **in-app** (already satisfied — `ProfileScreen.tsx` → `DELETE /api/auth/account`); Google needs it **as a URL**. Both are required; neither substitutes for the other.
  - the page's text must not drift from `docs/PRIVACY_DECLARATIONS.md`, `docs/store/play-health-connect-declaration.md` and the in-app consent copy — drift between them is itself a Health Connect rejection risk.
  - src: `compliance-auditor` 2026-08-26; hosting question re-verified against current Play terms 2026-08-27 · added: 2026-08-26
