It reverses `DEC-043`'s "stays marked" (the owner did not choose to hide the row in `DEC-122`), so it needs an owner ruling, and it reverses `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/experience/profile/__tests__/ProfileScreen.test.tsx:330` ("the row still renders"), which needs the owner's signature. If the owner ever rules that YouTube is not coming back, C comes at once rather than a rewording: "yet" is honest only while `DEC-043`'s intention stands (the `designer`, §4, INFO).
  - **Prerequisites, if the connect seal is lifted instead of D:** the 2026-10-01 brief's M7–M11 (one request that records the intent, revokes the refresh token at Google by a form-encoded POST and purges every YouTube-derived store, the user's sessions included whatever their provider; a daily sweep that finishes within 7 days; the build-race guard; revoke on account deletion; no token, body or query in any log); a hosted privacy policy meeting YouTube Developer Policies III.A.1 and III.A.2 (`DAN-015`); sensitive-scope verification, on an OAuth client chosen deliberately rather than the sign-in client's fallback (NF-17); `BE-060`; `BE-056`; `CMP-044`'s token re-check at least weekly, with deletion within 30 days of a revocation and no grace (NF-10); the pool cache keyed by the profile view (NR-2: `backend/app/services/selection/candidatePool.js:61-84` checks only `lastAnalyzed`, and `backend/app/workers/biometricBuffer.worker.js:17-25` builds pools from the unstripped profile under the same keys); the profile view keyed by provenance, not only by library rows (`musicProfileService.js:806-811`; the HX-3 backend developer, LOW); a `compliance-auditor`-cleared explanation, shown before the Disconnect action, of what it does (revoke at Google, delete within 7 days); a compare-and-set on the refreshed token (`backend/app/services/youtube.js:101-103` saves it on a possibly stale document) and a retention refresh that really re-validates the token (the architect's first-pass findings, P1 and P2); the retention worker's per-user lines made counts-only (`youtubeRetention.worker.js:95,99`); YouTube attribution wherever YouTube content reaches a screen (`CMP-041`, `UI-003`); and YouTube's written position on III.E.4.h(ii) and III.E.2.a (NF-12).

  - **update 2026-10-01 — the HX-3 round-1 reviews:**
    - **C is an open owner decision, not part of `DEC-122` (`compliance-auditor`, NF-B1, MEDIUM):** the owner did not choose "seal and hide", so `DEC-043`'s visible "Not yet available" row stands. `DEC-122`'s note is corrected (D-1), the PR body too (P-2), and the C bullet above now says C needs an owner ruling (the edit above). The title's "hide the YouTube row before the first iOS submission" is the brief's recommendation, to be put to the owner before that submission. So, for C, read "done when" as: the owner has ruled on C before the first iOS submission, and C has shipped before it if he ruled for it. The test the C bullet names is at `:331` on `fc62215` (`:330` on `bbae791`).
    - **Profile's linked YouTube row is a dead control under the seal (the `designer`, NEW-FINDING 1, P3):** under T3 a stored link always gets a 409 (`backend/app/controllers/integrationsController.js:428-441`), so each Disconnect tap shows "Could not disconnect YouTube" and "Please try again in a moment.", which invites retries that can never succeed, and the row keeps a green ✓ and "Connected" for a link Kokonada doesn't use (`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/experience/profile/ProfileScreen.tsx:122-126,342-348`). It does not block the `designer`'s SHIP, because I1 and I2 make it unreachable. Done when also, if the contingency fires: its rebuild replaces the row with either a working Disconnect behind a `compliance-auditor`-cleared explanation, or a cleared "remove access at Google" line, and drops the ✓ (A3, `MB-083`).
    - **NR-2's other half is the pool cache, not only the worker (`resilience-auditor`, NF2, LOW, accepted):** the view keeps `lastAnalyzed` (`backend/app/services/musicProfileService.js:810` spreads the profile), and `backend/app/services/selection/candidatePool.js:62,74` reuses any cached partition with the same stamp, for up to 12 h (`SELECTION_POOL_TTL_S`, default `12 * 3600`, `:24`). So a partition cached before the deploy from a profile holding YouTube rows would be reused; if translation then finds nothing playable, `backend/app/sockets/biometricHandler.js:1316` leaves `merged` as it was, and `:1038-1040` record its YouTube ids and keys in `PlaylistSession` and the serve ledger. Reachable only if YouTube profiles existed at the deploy: P0 counted 0, and `DAN-046` P1 re-checks. The prerequisite above, the pool cache keyed by the profile view, covers it.
    - **the mixed-profile footprint (`resilience-auditor`, NF3, INFO):** for a mixed Spotify and YouTube profile, `withoutYoutube` re-derives the Spotify footprint from the library rows, not from Spotify's own analysis; Spotify rows often carry no genres, so the view's genre set can come out empty and personalisation thin. Mixed profiles cannot form under the seal. Recorded for any unseal.
    - **the provenance LOW, confirmed (`resilience-auditor`, NF4):** `withoutYoutube` keys on library rows (`musicProfileService.js:806-811`), so subscription artists and topic genres merged into the top artists and top genres survive it with zero YouTube rows, for example when the classifier drops every video, and P0's count of "profiles with YouTube rows" cannot see that shape. It needs a current token (0 at P0) or a past disconnect; the old disconnect handler either rebuilt from Spotify or `$set` the library and all three footprint lists to `[]`, so that residue cannot exist today. The prerequisite above, the view keyed by provenance, stands.
    - **confirmed for the record (`compliance-auditor`):** the HX-3 backend developer's three LOW points parked here (provenance keying, `NO_PLAYABLE_PROVIDER_MESSAGE`, and the unstripped pool in `biometricBuffer.worker.js`) are correctly parked; all three are unreachable under the seal.
    - src: `compliance-auditor`'s HX-3 round-1 review (§6, ARCH-012 and D-1; NF-B1; NEW-FINDING, confirmed), the `designer`'s (NEW-FINDING 1) and the `resilience-auditor`'s at `fc62215` (NF2, NF3, NF4), 2026-10-01; the lines re-checked by the scribe on `fc62215`.

  - added: 2026-08-26
- [x] `DAN-002` `P1` **Console cleanup before the Vercel teardown — the numbered runbook is below. The ORDER is the whole point and it costs nothing.** `#blocked` → `BE-001`, `BE-003`, `ARCH-002`, `DAN-007`
  - **CLOSED ON EVIDENCE 2026-09-23 — Daniel ran steps 1, 3, 4 (FRONTEND_URL deleted), 5 and 6.** Verified from outside: `kokonada-frontend.vercel.app` and `kokonada.vercel.app` both return 404, `/health` returns 200 without `FRONTEND_URL`, and `gh secret list` shows no `VERCEL_*` secret. Step 1 (Apple) was not applicable: there is no Apple Developer account yet, so no web Services ID or Return URL ever existed. The one Apple action left moved to `DAN-011`.
  - **UPDATE 2026-09-23 — the hard dependency below is SATISFIED.** `BE-001` and `BE-003` both landed in PR #187, so the runbook can now be run start to finish. Still open: step 3 (verify a real YouTube connect) has no mobile caller until `MB-008` lands.
  - ### ⛔ HARD DEPENDENCY — `BE-001` AND `BE-003` MUST LAND BEFORE ANY VERCEL TEARDOWN
    The OAuth completion path still has a `'web'` fallback that redirects to `${FRONTEND_URL}/integrations?…` (`integrationsController.js:41`), reached whenever a signed state is unreadable or tampered (`_returnTarget`'s catch) and **unconditionally by every Garmin callback** (`BE-003` — Garmin is the one provider with no app branch on any path). Tear the host down while those still point at it and you get precisely the failure this whole effort exists to prevent: `res.redirect` emits a **relative** `Location` resolved against the Railway origin, a **silent 404 with no exception and no log line** (`BE-001`, `P0`). Nothing goes red. Nobody finds out.
    **Order is therefore: `BE-001` + `BE-003` land → then this runbook → then the teardown.** Not the other way around.
  - ### 🔄 REVERSAL, stated plainly so step 3 is not followed blindly
    Daniel's earlier ruling *"KEEP `FRONTEND_URL`"* **was correct when made and is now superseded.** It was correct because `BE-001`/`BE-003` had not landed, so `frontendRedirect` was still a live reader. **Once they land, the variable has no readers at all** — and with `cors()` removed the `index.js:46` boot throw goes with it, so nothing reads it and nothing requires it. **Step 3 below then becomes "DELETE `FRONTEND_URL`", not "repoint it".** Re-read this before running step 3; which version applies depends on whether `BE-001`/`BE-003` are in `main` at the time.
  - **Severity re-rated P0 → P1 on evidence, 2026-08-26.** Two independent mitigations, neither of which removes the ordering requirement:
    - **No victim population.** Nothing is in production, the app is unpublished on both stores (`DAN-003`), and there are no real users. A dangling Return URL harms someone only when a real person completes a web Apple sign-in through it. Nobody does.
    - **The takeover mechanism is UNCONFIRMED, and the evidence leans against it.** `#inconclusive` — Vercel's generated-URL docs (`vercel.com/docs/deployments/generated-urls`) describe how `<project-name>-<scope-slug>.vercel.app` names are *formed* and say nothing about a name being released or reusable after project deletion. Multiple independent community reports say the opposite of "free pool": after deletion the name becomes **stuck/orphaned** and even the *original owner* cannot reclaim it, some receiving "already assigned to another team". No published security research shows a bare `*.vercel.app` name being claimed by a third party post-deletion; documented Vercel takeovers are **custom** domains with dangling CNAMEs, which is a different mechanism. Vercel publishes no official policy either way, so this is not refuted — only unsupported.
  - **What is NOT mitigated, and why the ordering stands anyway:** the exposure window opens at whichever comes first — the Vercel project being deleted, or the first real user. Doing the console steps first closes it before either can happen, at a cost of about five minutes. Do them in order and this is a chore; skip the order and it is an incident that needs Apple's audit log to unpick.
  - **Worst realistic case if the mechanism does turn out to be live:** an attacker who controls the host and holds a valid Apple `id_token` minted for our Services ID could present it to `authController.js:25-32`, which verifies only `aud == APPLE_CLIENT_ID`. That is unauthorised *account creation/access as themselves*, not takeover of a specific existing user — takeover additionally requires phishing a real user through the dangling host, and there are none.
  - **There is no Facebook console** — verified, no live Facebook registration exists anywhere in the repo.
  - **TWO VERCEL PROJECTS, TWO DOMAINS, BOTH SERVING THE SAME DEAD WEB APP — BOTH GET DELETED (confirmed by Daniel 2026-08-27).** `kokonada-frontend` → `kokonada-frontend.vercel.app` (live, Google sign-in works, so it holds a real `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`); `kokonada` → `kokonada.vercel.app` (same app, sign-in dies with *"Google Sign-In is not configured for this build (missing VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID)"* — a stale duplicate). Deleting only one leaves the web alive at the other address and the teardown was theatre.
  - **WHICH ORIGIN IS REGISTERED WHERE — repo evidence, and its limit.** `kokonada.vercel.app` appears **nowhere in this repository** — not in any doc, env file, test or source. Every configured reference is to `kokonada-frontend.vercel.app`: `docs/DEPLOYMENT_OAUTH_CONFIG.md:8,24,68,93` (the paste-ready console values), `frontend/src/__tests__/LoginPage.sameOriginAuth.test.tsx:39` (the Apple Return URL), and `backend/.env:36`. The only other origin anywhere is `https://app.kokonada.com`, which exists solely as a **test fixture** in `backend/tests/csrf.test.js:3,27,32` and was never real. **So the expectation is that you are removing `kokonada-frontend` entries only.**
    **But do not take that as proof the `kokonada` origin was never registered.** Its sign-in failure is a *missing build-time env var on that Vercel project* — it says nothing about what is in the Google console. The console list is the authority, not the repo and not the error message. While you are in each console, read the list and remove **anything naming either host**.
  - **CONFIRMED DEFECT — `YOUTUBE_REDIRECT_URI` on Railway points at the web app and must be repointed BEFORE the teardown.** Daniel verified in the Railway console 2026-08-27 that it is set to `https://kokonada-frontend.vercel.app/auth/youtube/callback` — a **frontend page path**. YouTube connect breaks the moment Vercel dies. This is drift: `backend/.env.example:42` has always prescribed the backend path, and `docs/DEPLOYMENT_OAUTH_CONFIG.md:35` already records the correct production value.
    **Exact replacement value, read from code and ready to paste:**
    ```
    YOUTUBE_REDIRECT_URI=https://kokonada-backend-production.up.railway.app/api/integrations/youtube/callback
    ```
    **Confirmed against the real handler, not inferred from the URL shape:** the integrations router is mounted at `/api/integrations` (`backend/app/index.js:98`) and the callback is `router.get('/youtube/callback', youtubeCallback)` (`backend/app/routes/integrations.js:34`) — so `/api/integrations/youtube/callback` is the true path. Spotify's already-working value has the identical shape (`DEPLOYMENT_OAUTH_CONFIG.md:29`).
    **One value drives both legs:** `backend/app/services/youtube.js:25` `redirectUri()` is used at `:46` (the auth URL) and `:62` (the token exchange), so changing the variable changes both atomically and they cannot drift apart. An OAuth flow already in flight across the change would fail — irrelevant with no users.
  - **CORRECTED ORDERING FOR THE REDIRECT REPOINT — Daniel's sequence is right and is now the runbook.** Google OAuth clients accept **multiple** authorized redirect URIs, and the one sent must exactly match a registered entry; there is no exclusivity, so adding before switching is safe and removes the broken window entirely:
    1. **Google console → add** `https://kokonada-backend-production.up.railway.app/api/integrations/youtube/callback` to **Authorized redirect URIs**. Leave the old Vercel entry in place. Google warns changes can take *5 minutes to a few hours* to propagate — wait before step 2.
    2. **Railway → Variables →** set `YOUTUBE_REDIRECT_URI` to the value above. Let the service redeploy.
    3. **Verify** a real YouTube connect completes end to end (see `MB-008` first — there may be no client able to start one).
    4. **Only then remove** the old `…vercel.app/auth/youtube/callback` entry from the Google console.
    "Repoint then remove" as one step is unsafe; "add, switch, verify, remove" is the correct order. (`backend/.env` is your gitignored local file; only that one line was read.)
  - **Worth knowing while this is parked: `kokonada-frontend` has a WORKING sign-in right now**, so there is a live public surface issuing exactly the `SameSite=None` session cookie that `BE-008` (CSWSH) and `BE-009` (cookie retirement) are about. Not urgent with no users — but the teardown and the cookie retirement are closing the same door from two sides, and whichever lands first narrows the other.

  **1 · Apple Developer — retire the web Return URL.** developer.apple.com → Account → Certificates, Identifiers & Profiles → **Identifiers** → switch the filter (top right) from *App IDs* to **Services IDs** → open the Kokonada web Services ID (its identifier looks like `com.kokonadahealth.web` or similar — it is *not* the iOS bundle id).

  - Web sign-in is not coming back, so **delete the Services ID entirely** once the Return URL is gone (Identifiers → select it → Remove).
  - **Then set backend `APPLE_CLIENT_ID` to the iOS bundle id `com.kokonadahealth`** (Railway → backend service → Variables). It can hold only one value, and the native flow's `aud` is the bundle id (`mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/auth/auth.ts:69-82` sends `platform:'ios'`). If it currently holds the web Services ID, native Sign in with Apple will fail verification the moment iOS is testable — and a broken Sign in with Apple is an App Store 4.8 rejection, not just a bug.
  - **Verify:** the Services ID no longer appears under Identifiers, and Railway shows `APPLE_CLIENT_ID=com.kokonadahealth`.

  **2 · Google Cloud — drop the dead JavaScript origins.** console.cloud.google.com → the Kokonada project → **APIs & Services → Credentials** → open the OAuth 2.0 Client ID used for login (the one starting `225621926146-…`).

  - **Leave Authorized redirect URIs alone** — the `…up.railway.app/api/integrations/{youtube,spotify}/callback` entries are backend-hosted and still in use. Removing them breaks YouTube connect.
  - **Verify:** the origins list is empty or contains only origins you own; then confirm a Google login still works from the mobile app (it uses the native SDK, not a browser origin, so it should be unaffected — that is the point of checking).

  **3 · CORRECTED 2026-09-23 — DELETE `FRONTEND_URL` on Railway; the warning below is obsolete.** PR #187 removed the boot assertion and the last reader, so the service boots without it. Verify `/health` returns 200 after the redeploy.
  ~~**3 · Railway — repoint `FRONTEND_URL`, never unset it.**~~ railway.app → the backend service → **Variables**.

Until `kokonada.app` exists (`DAN-007`), any stable placeholder you control is fine — **but do not delete the variable**: `backend/app/index.js:46-48` throws `FRONTEND_URL must be set in production — refusing to start with unsafe CORS` and the service will not boot.

- [x] `DAN-008` `P0` **CLOSED 2026-09-01 — ANSWERED FROM SOURCE. React Native sends NO `Origin` header, and the question is moot anyway.** No on-device capture was needed; the row's premise that this "cannot be settled from source" was wrong, because it looked only at our JS and not at the platform stack it suspected.
  - **Proof, RN 0.86.0 vendored in `mobile/KokonadaHealth/node_modules/react-native`:** a grep for `'Origin'` across `Libraries/Network/` returns **nothing** — no RN code path sets the header. And `Libraries/Network/XMLHttpRequest.js` carries **no CORS model and no forbidden-header list** at all (only a `withCredentials` flag, which governs cookie attachment, not `Origin`). In a browser `Origin` is a *forbidden header name* set by the user-agent; RN has no user-agent and no browsing context, so nothing sets it. `mobile/.../net/apiClient.ts:36` sets only `Content-Type` and `Authorization: Bearer`.
  - **So inverting the guard to deny-no-Origin would 403 the entire mobile app** — exactly the outcome this row existed to prevent. Do not invert it. See `ARCH-002`.
  - **And the guard's premise is void regardless.** `middleware/csrf.js`'s own comment says it *"complements the SameSite=None auth cookie (which alone is CSRF-fragile)"*. **PR #184 retired that cookie** (`BE-008`/`BE-009`): auth is `Authorization: Bearer` only, and the socket handshake takes `auth.token` only. CSRF requires an **ambient** credential the browser attaches by itself; a Bearer header is never ambient, so a forged cross-site request now arrives with no credential at all. The remaining `?ct=` path is an explicit, signed, single-use query token scoped to one OAuth navigation — also not ambient. ~~ON-DEVICE CAPTURE: does React Native send an `Origin` header?~~ **(original row below, kept for the record)**
- [x] ~~`DAN-008` original brief~~ **ON-DEVICE CAPTURE: does React Native send an `Origin` header?** `BE-009`/`ARCH-002` want the CSRF guard inverted to deny-all-origins. If RN sets an `Origin` on POST, that inversion **403s the entire mobile app**. This cannot be settled from source — `mobile/.../net/apiClient.ts:35-37` sets only `Content-Type` and `Authorization`, but the header may be added by the platform networking stack below JS. Nothing that can 403 the whole app ships on "React Native probably does X". → `BE-009`
  - **This touches NO repo code.** It is a 20-line throwaway logger the phone talks to instead of the real backend.
  - **1 · Save this as `C:\Users\danie\Desktop\hdrlog.js`:**
    ```js
    const http = require('http');
    http.createServer((req, res) => {
      let body = '';
      req.on('data', c => body += c);
      req.on('end', () => {
        console.log('\n=== ' + req.method + ' ' + req.url);
        console.log('  origin : ' + (req.headers.origin  ?? '<ABSENT>'));
        console.log('  cookie : ' + (req.headers.cookie  ?? '<ABSENT>'));
        console.log('  auth   : ' + (req.headers.authorization ? 'Bearer present' : '<ABSENT>'));
        console.log('  referer: ' + (req.headers.referer ?? '<ABSENT>'));
        console.log('  ua     : ' + (req.headers['user-agent'] ?? '<ABSENT>'));
        res.writeHead(200, {'Content-Type':'application/json'});
        res.end('{"ok":false,"error":"capture-stub"}');
      });
    }).listen(5000, () => console.log('listening on :5000 — waiting for the app'));
    ```
  - **2 · Run it:** `node C:\Users\danie\Desktop\hdrlog.js` — leave the window open.
  - **3 · Point the phone at it:** `adb reverse tcp:5000 tcp:5000` (adb lives at `C:\Users\danie\AppData\Local\Android\Sdk\platform-tools\adb.exe`). Then set `BACKEND_URL` to `http://localhost:5000` in `mobile/KokonadaHealth/src/health/config.ts` — **local edit only, revert it afterwards, do not commit.** Rebuild/reload the app.
  - **4 · Make the app POST.** Open the app and tap anything that hits the server — Sign in is ideal (`POST /api/auth/google`), and any generate/consent action also works. The request will fail; that is fine, we only need the headers.
  - **5 · WHAT TO LOOK FOR, and this is the whole answer:** in the terminal, the `origin :` line on any **POST**.
    - `origin : <ABSENT>` → **the inverted deny-all guard is SAFE to ship.** RN sends no Origin, so `csrf.js:24`'s fail-open keeps letting the app through while every real browser origin gets a 403.
    - `origin : <anything>` → **STOP. Do not ship the inverted guard** — it would 403 the app. Tell me the exact value and I will redesign around it (allowlist that value, or gate on the Bearer plane instead).
    - Also report the `cookie :` line — if it ever shows `kokonada_token=…`, the RN app is holding a cookie in a native jar, which changes `BE-009` step 1.
  - **6 · Clean up:** Ctrl-C the logger, `adb reverse --remove tcp:5000`, revert the `config.ts` edit (`git diff` it to be sure), rebuild.

- [ ] `DAN-007` `P2` **Buy `kokonada.app`** (or another apex domain you own and can add a DNS TXT record to), then verify it in Google Search Console as a **Domain property (DNS-level)** and add it as an Authorized Domain on the OAuth consent screen. Registering a domain is a portal action only Daniel can take.
  - **SCOPE CORRECTED 2026-08-27 — this no longer blocks the landing page, but it is NOT merely branding.** The two requirements were conflated and they are separate:
    - **Store URLs (Play deletion URL, privacy policy, support) — do NOT need this.** No ownership or verification requirement exists in the Play terms; `*.github.io` is acceptable. `ARCH-003` is unblocked and can ship today.
    - **Google OAuth verification — DOES need this, and `github.io` cannot satisfy it.** Verbatim: *"You must verify the **Domain Property (DNS-level)**, rather than a 'URL prefix' or 'Site,' property."* — [support.google.com/cloud/answer/13804266](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13804266); and *"The homepage must be hosted on a verified domain you own"* — [support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464321](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464321). A `*.github.io` subdomain sits under a Public Suffix List domain whose DNS zone we do not control, so no TXT record can be added and no Domain property can be created for it.
  - **What it still gates:** OAuth verification for the `youtube.readonly` sensitive scope. Unverified, the app is capped at *"100 new users in total, after the app presents the unverified app screen"*. So this is a real ceiling on YouTube-connected users, not cosmetics — it simply is not on the critical path for submission-blocking store URLs any more. Dropped `P1` → `P2` on that basis.

  - **why it must be an owned apex domain, not a subdomain of someone else's:** Google requires the OAuth homepage on a DNS-verified *Domain Property* — *"You must verify the Domain Property (DNS-level), rather than a 'URL prefix' or 'Site,' property."* Neither `kokonada-frontend.vercel.app` nor `kokonada-backend-production.up.railway.app` can ever satisfy that, because we do not control those DNS zones. So a landing page on the backend as a static route, or rebuilt on the same Vercel host, **does not work** — this is not a hosting preference, it is a hard constraint.
  - **what it blocks:** the landing page has no legal home until it exists (`ARCH-003`), and the landing page is what the Play **account-deletion URL** and **privacy-policy URL** will point at — both mandatory, both currently non-existent. Also gates: the in-app privacy link both stores require; the Health Connect rationale WebView, which today shows the user *Google's own developer docs* (`PermissionsRationaleActivity.kt:19`); and Google OAuth verification, without which `youtube.readonly` is capped at 100 total users.

  - **Write the code anyway where it is a latent landmine, not a feature.** The deregistration fix is worth landing unbuilt-against: after `User.deleteOne` the credentials are gone and the registration can *never* be cleaned up, so the bug is unrecoverable-by-construction rather than merely dormant.

    - **The ™ question moves here too:** the guidelines carry no marking rule; it may be in the non-public Consumer Brand Style Guide (`UI-016`).
    - **The Garmin Connect app icon** is licensee-only, at least 88 px and must not be altered, and downloading it requires accepting the Confidentiality Agreement `#verify` — the auditor's reading; the guideline text says only "use the full app name and tile" (`txt:3-4`). It gates `MB-005` and `OPS-041`.
    - **Table row 1 is wrong twice:** the canvas "Garmin · live" tile (now at `docs/canvas/Main.dc.html:57` and `docs/review/06-generate.html:299`) has no device model, where the guidelines require "Garmin [device model]" and allow bare "Garmin" only when the API gives no model (`txt:30-32`); and "live" is untrue, since no Garmin ingest runs.
  - **update 2026-09-29 — the Developer Program Agreement is public after all (`compliance-auditor`, L1 provider registry, preamble), which contradicts this row's "not public" and "no agreement to be bound by".** FRM-0952 Rev. B exists in two variants, both read on 2026-09-29. The Garmin International, Inc. variant (14 pages) has no §6.4–§6.6, no definition of "Garmin Device Sourced Data" and no clause on artificial intelligence. The Garmin China Shanghai RHQ variant (19 pages, PDF created 2026-03-03) has the 30-day notice (§6.6) and an AI transparency clause (§15.10), and defines that data as data made available "via the API or Garmin Connect Developer Program", so under it Garmin-origin data read through Health Connect does not trigger the notice. Both variants limit data use "only to the extent necessary to format and display such data" (§4.1(b)(ii)), which puts Garmin data as a music-generation input in question (`CMP-063`). Garmin's FAQ says the program "is only for business use", and the `sentinel` adds that the public FAQ shows no pause in issuing accounts (L1 calendar, C7). Registry: `CMP-051`, `CMP-052`, `CMP-060`, `CMP-063`, `CMP-064`. Owner questions: `DEC-044` (the entity), `DEC-045` (which variant, and when to serve the notice), `DEC-046` (the tile), `DEC-047` (Garmin data as a generation input) and `DEC-048` (the §15.10 statement). The two PDFs are saved only as temporary tool files (`cmp-compliance.md`, "Relevant paths"); copy them to `Kokonada-private/garmin/` to keep them. `#verify` — the agreement text was not re-read by the scribe.

  |---|---|---|---|

No ingest path exists; the lane is pre-production and gated. **Honest fix:** the `garmin` prop value is an end-state variant and should be labelled as one, exactly like `Connect.dc.html`'s `intended` chip. |
  | 2 | SpO₂ / respiration / Body Battery™ *"if you connect a Garmin account"* | `Consent.dc.html` ALSO COVERED, `ConsentSheet.tsx:27-29` | **Honest already** — it says "None is read today; we ask again first", and `garminConsentVersionGate` *enforces* the dormancy. This is the pattern the rest should copy: disclose-before-read, gated in code, stated in copy. Keep it. |
  | 3 | A pairable **Connect IQ watch app** — pairing-code UI, `whr_` device tokens, a live-HR card | `WatchPairingCard.tsx`, `watchPairingStore.ts`, `watchPairingClient.ts`, `liveHrClient.ts`, `watch/` | Live on `main` **and** in this branch. Already being retired by the other session's in-flight PR #181 (`8683c93` — replaces it with `LiveHeartRateCard` and adds `surfaceBoundaryGuard.test.js`), **not merged anywhere yet.** Nothing to do here beyond letting it land; note that a Connect IQ store listing would need its own agreement and its own audit regardless. |
